**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 builder transport doctor truth landed.** `mvmctl doctor` now makes the builder-path networking shape explicit instead of forcing an operator to infer it from the backend choice plus the separate egress line. The new informational `builder transport` platform check reports HVF as the vsock-only builder path with no guest NIC or DHCP/bootstrap dependency, flags libkrun as the remaining legacy guest-network bootstrap exception, and marks qemu as an unsupported legacy builder fallback outside the production architecture claim. This closes the remaining Phase 2C `doctor` distinction between workload transport truth, builder transport truth, and unsupported legacy paths. Validation is green with `cargo fmt --all`, `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test --offline -p mvm-cli --lib builder_transport_check -- --test-threads=1`, and `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy --offline -p mvm-cli --lib -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 tunnel-worker doctor state landed.** `mvmctl doctor` now exposes the workload packet-tunnel worker as an informational platform check instead of leaving the new host-authority path opaque. It scans `~/.mvm/vms/` and distinguishes four real states from persisted runtime artifacts: `absent`, live `drop_all`, live `l3_forward (<host-tun-iface>)`, and `stale` tunnel-worker leftovers. The check reuses the backend’s exported state-file names (`network-tunnel-worker.pid`, `network-tunnel.audit.jsonl`, `network-tunnel-host-iface`) so the runtime path and the operator surface cannot drift. Validation is green with `cargo fmt --all`, `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test --offline -p mvm-cli --lib network_tunnel_worker_summary -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test --offline -p mvm-backend --lib network_tunnel_spawn -- --test-threads=1`, `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy --offline -p mvm-cli --lib -- -D warnings`, and `cargo clippy --offline -p mvm-backend --lib -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Builder relay runtime evidence cleanup contract pinned.** The dead-builder orphan reaper in `mvm-cli` already removed whole ephemeral builder state dirs under `~/.cache/mvm/builder-vm/vms/`, which means the new `builder-egress-runtime.json` sidecar was cleaned up implicitly. This slice makes that guarantee explicit instead of relying on reader inference: the reaper contract now documents whole-dir removal for per-run evidence sidecars, and the focused reaper regression stages a real `builder-egress-runtime.json` under a dead builder dir and proves the dir is swept. Validation is green with `cargo fmt --all`, `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo test --offline -p mvm-cli --lib dead_pids_get_their_dirs_swept -- --test-threads=1`, and `MVM_SKIP_EMBED_BINARIES=1 cargo clippy --offline -p mvm-cli --lib -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 226 WS-B HVF dev default is now back in place.** The macOS-26 `mvmctl dev` path no longer stays libkrun-first after the Vz deletion slice: `dev up` now prefers HVF when the detached `mvm-hvf-supervisor` helper is actually launchable, libkrun remains the fallback, and the follow-up verbs (`dev shell`, `dev status`, `dev down`, `dev rebuild`) route through the backend that owns the running dev VM instead of assuming one backend. The HVF path now also waits for guest-agent readiness before declaring success. Closing this left a small reliability tail in the workspace gates, all fixed in the same branch: the sealed-image `invoke` admission test now uses an isolated temp data dir, transcript-capture bridge tests inject explicit transcript/key roots instead of mutating `MVM_DATA_DIR`, the Stage 0 bootstrap in-flight probe retries briefly before failing safe to "busy", and `libkrun-sys` disables doctests so the full workspace test gate no longer trips in the bindings crate. Validation is green with `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test --workspace` on the worktree.
**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A guest tunnel runtime tightened.** Tunnel-enabled boots no longer lean on the legacy guest-NIC path: `mvm-guest-netinit` now detects the shared `mvm.network_tunnel=` cmdline token and skips the old `eth0` bring-up when the raw packet tunnel is requested, so the guest-side bootstrap does not quietly prepare a parallel host-network route. The guest loop in `mvm_guest::network_tunnel` now also treats host control frames as control frames during steady-state operation: keepalive/credit frames are ignored, and a host `SHUTDOWN` frame ends the loop cleanly instead of surfacing as a packet-frame decode failure. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A runtime-owned tunnel worker subprocess started.** The shared packet-tunnel port is now backed by a real host-owned helper instead of a bare standing socket contract. `mvm-network-tunnel-worker` binds either the per-VM host UDS or a host AF_VSOCK listener, accepts one guest tunnel stream, reuses the shared `HELLO` / `HELLO_ACK` / `CONFIG` bootstrap plus `HostTunnelWorker`, and appends JSONL audit events. `mvm-backend::network_tunnel_spawn` now owns the backend-neutral spawn/reap seam, helper lookup, host-authored default `mvm-net0` config, initial bounded `CREDIT` grant, listener selection, readiness polling, and fail-closed pid/socket lifecycle. The HVF workload runner and libkrun backend keep the existing per-port UDS listener shape, while Firecracker now carries the shared tunnel config in `FlakeRunConfig`, appends the shared `mvm.network_tunnel=` cmdline token, and starts/reaps the same worker on a host AF_VSOCK listener for the configured guest port. On the guest side, the runtime now retains one pending packet when send credit is exhausted instead of draining the TUN indefinitely, and resumes forwarding only after a later host `CREDIT` update replenishes that bounded budget. This is still default-deny and pre-forwarding, but it means the optional tunnel port now has the first real backpressure contract that later WHP adapters and admitted forwarders can reuse instead of inventing another worker/session shape. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-core network_tunnel --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib`, and clippy `-D warnings` for `mvm-core`, `mvm-guest`, `mvm-hostd`, and `mvm-backend`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A host default-deny tunnel worker started.** `mvm_hostd::network_tunnel::HostTunnelWorker` now turns the shared session/bootstrap seam into the first real host-owned packet worker for the raw L3 tunnel path: it accepts the validated `HELLO` / `HELLO_ACK` / `CONFIG` bootstrap, owns per-session packet and byte counters, applies an explicit `DropAll` packet policy, records audit events for bootstrap/drop/close, and fails closed with shared `ERROR` + `SHUTDOWN` control frames when packet quotas are exceeded. This is still pre-forwarding, but it means the host side is no longer just a bare socket contract; Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP now have one reusable host-worker loop for default-deny handling before admitted forwarding lands. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A host TUN packet-device seam landed.** `mvm-hostd` now exposes `host_tun::HostTunDevice`, the first host-owned Linux `/dev/net/tun` primitive for the packet-tunnel path. It validates interface names before any syscall, binds a named `IFF_TUN | IFF_NO_PI` device with the target ioctl type, and exposes blocking packet read/write plus raw-fd access for later host-owned L3 forwarders. That keeps the future admitted forwarding path backend-neutral: HVF, libkrun, Firecracker, and later WHP can all reuse one host packet-device seam instead of each backend growing its own TUN open/bind logic around the session worker. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-hostd host_tun --lib -- --test-threads=1` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A host packet-path worker seam landed.** `mvm_hostd::network_tunnel::HostTunnelWorker` no longer assumes the only safe host behavior is "count then drop." It now has a pluggable packet-path seam: the default `run_until_shutdown()` path still uses explicit `DropAll`, but `run_until_shutdown_with_packet_path()` lets a later admitted forwarder reuse the same bootstrap, quota, audit, and fail-closed control loop. The first concrete adapter is `HostTunPacketPath<D>`, which writes guest packets into a host-owned TUN device and records forwarded-packet audit/stats. Short writes or device failures now shut the tunnel down explicitly with `ERROR internal` plus `SHUTDOWN host_error` instead of silently truncating traffic. This is still pre-return-path and not yet the full admitted forwarder, but it moves the host worker from a single-policy sink toward the intended L3 packet-tunnel architecture without tying that step to one backend. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-core network_tunnel --lib -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib -- --test-threads=1`, and clippy `-D warnings` for `mvm-core` and `mvm-hostd`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A host bidirectional TUN relay substrate landed.** The same host-owned tunnel worker now has the first real blocking relay loop over both directions of the raw packet path. `HostTunnelWorker::run_blocking_tun_relay_loop()` polls the guest tunnel session fd and a `HostTunPacketPath` fd together, forwards guest packets into the host-owned device, and delivers host-originated packets back to the guest as raw packet frames while reusing the same bootstrap, quota, audit, and fail-closed shutdown behavior. The new unit witness proves both directions end to end with no guest NIC, no bridge, and no semantic DNS/TCP/UDP flow layer in the middle. This is still not the final admitted production forwarder, but it is the first concrete host-side return-path substrate on the active Plan 236 line. Focused validation is green with `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo test -p mvm-core network_tunnel --lib -- --test-threads=1`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib -- --test-threads=1`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A guest tunnel pump-loop substrate started.** `mvm_guest::network_tunnel::BootstrappedGuestTunnel` now has a testable `pump_ready` tick and a Linux-only `run_blocking_packet_loop` that polls the guest TUN fd and tunnel session fd together. This is the first real runtime loop model for the raw L3 tunnel path: readiness-driven, bounded per tick, and still packet-carriage-based rather than a semantic DNS/TCP/UDP flow synthesizer. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 transparent-net return-path review recorded.** The `vsock-transparent-net-return-path` worktree does not change the active Plan 236 architecture line; it reinforces it. The review conclusion is now explicit in the roadmap: reuse bounded pump loops, strict size limits, and fail-closed vsock relays from the exploratory transparent-net stack, but do not import its semantic DNS/TCP/UDP protocol or the host/guest return-path packet-synthesis model into the Phase 2 raw L3 packet tunnel baseline.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A guest packet-relay helper started.** `mvm_guest::network_tunnel::BootstrappedGuestTunnel` now covers the first bounded packet movement across the backend-agnostic tunnel seam: pump one packet from the guest TUN into the tunnel session and one packet from the tunnel session back into the guest TUN, reusing a generic packet-device trait so the relay logic is testable without a real `/dev/net/tun`. Both directions enforce the negotiated frame size and fail closed on oversize payloads or short writes instead of silently truncating traffic. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A guest TUN device helper started.** `mvm_guest::guest_tun` now provides the first real guest-side data-plane primitive for the shared tunnel path: open `/dev/net/tun`, bind the host-authored interface name with `TUNSETIFF`, and exchange raw packets via blocking `Read` / `Write`. `mvm_guest::network_tunnel::BootstrappedGuestTunnel::prepare_tun_device` now ties that device open to the shared `CONFIG`-application step, so the future packet pump can start from one backend-agnostic bootstrap result instead of inventing another guest-NIC-shaped bring-up path. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest guest_tun --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A shared tunnel bootstrap helper started.** The guest and host tunnel helpers now cover the full bootstrap exchange instead of stopping at `HELLO`: `mvm_guest::network_tunnel` exposes `bootstrap_over_stream` / `bootstrap_from_cmdline` to complete `HELLO` / `HELLO_ACK` / `CONFIG` and retain the live session plus host-authored guest config, while `mvm_hostd::network_tunnel` exposes `send_network_config` / `accept_bootstrap` so backend adapters can reuse one host-owned bootstrap sequence. This is still pre-packet-pump, but it gives Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP one backend-agnostic session bootstrap shape before the live guest TUN and host worker loops land. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A guest tunnel network-config helper started.** `mvm_guest::network_tunnel` can now receive the host `CONFIG` frame as a typed `TunnelNetworkConfig`, and `mvm_guest::guest_net` now exposes one Linux-only `configure_tunnel_guest_network` helper that reuses the existing ioctl-based guest bring-up path for MTU, static IPv4, route, and resolver staging. This is still pre-packet-pump guest work, but it turns the shared tunnel contract into concrete backend-agnostic guest runtime behavior for Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP without reintroducing a guest-NIC-shaped dependency. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest guest_net --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A tunnel cmdline/bootstrap wiring started.** The shared tunnel runtime config now crosses the sealed-guest boot boundary via a dedicated `mvm.network_tunnel=<hex(JSON)>` cmdline token: `mvm_core::vm_backend` owns the encode/decode helpers, the HVF and libkrun workload cmdline builders emit the token when a tunnel runtime config is present, and `mvm_guest::network_tunnel` can recover that config from `/proc/cmdline` and turn it into the validated guest `HELLO`. This keeps the boot-metadata → handshake mapping backend-agnostic and avoids another round of per-backend identity/session reconstruction before the live TUN and host-worker packet pumps land. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-core encode_network_tunnel_cmdline --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib`, the focused HVF/libkrun cmdline tests, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-core -p mvm-guest -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A tunnel launch-carrier wiring started.** The shared tunnel contract now includes `TunnelSessionConfig` and `TunnelRuntimeConfig`, `VmStartConfig` carries the optional packet-tunnel runtime carrier explicitly, the workload-role standing-vsock map can expose the optional guest-dials tunnel socket from that shared config, and `mvm_hostd::network_tunnel` can derive `ExpectedTunnelSession` from the same shared session config. This is still pre-packet-pump plumbing, but it removes another backend-specific integration seam so Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP can all consume one launch-time tunnel identity/socket contract. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-core network_tunnel --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-backend workload_runner::spec_map --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-core -p mvm-backend -p mvm-hostd -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A host tunnel-session helper started.** `mvm_hostd::network_tunnel` now provides the host-side blocking session seam for the shared contract: validate the first guest `HELLO` against host-owned `tenant_id` / `vm_id` / `boot_id` / `session_nonce`, emit the matching `HELLO_ACK`, and exchange typed control or packet frames over any backend-provided blocking stream. This keeps Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP on one reusable host identity/session boundary instead of each backend inventing its own handshake checks. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-hostd network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A shared tunnel contract started.** The first reusable implementation slice for the host-authority data plane is now in `mvm_core::protocol::network_tunnel`: a backend-agnostic guest-TUN ↔ host-worker tunnel contract with a fixed bounded frame header, whole-frame encode/decode helpers, typed control payloads (`HELLO`, `HELLO_ACK`, `CONFIG`, `CREDIT`, `HEARTBEAT`, `ERROR`, `SHUTDOWN`), session-bound identity fields (`tenant_id`, `vm_id`, `boot_id`, `session_nonce`), guest interface/DNS/MTU config validation, and fail-closed parsing. This is intentionally not an HVF-only shortcut: the wire surface is designed for Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP to share while each backend keeps its own transport adapter and capability truth. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-core network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-core --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 Phase 2A guest tunnel-session helper started.** `mvm_guest::network_tunnel` now wraps the shared contract in a guest-side blocking session helper: reuse the existing guest→host AF_VSOCK dial path, negotiate `HELLO` / `HELLO_ACK`, send/receive typed control frames, and send/receive packet frames with the same shared fail-closed decode path. This is still pre-TUN plumbing, but it removes the need for each backend path to invent another guest framing/session layer and keeps the transport reusable for Firecracker, libkrun, HVF, and future backends such as WHP. Focused validation is green with `cargo test -p mvm-guest network_tunnel --lib` and `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 237 Phase 0 first PR implemented, clean density baseline pending.** The first slice stays deliberately pre-optimization: the shared guest network helper used by `mvm-oci-init` uses the target musl ioctl request type for loopback bring-up, stale OCI cache records can repair a missing rootfs output from the still-present unpacked layer tree, and `scripts/measure-hvf-density.sh` captures isolated HVF density artifacts outside the repository while preserving the current foreground `mvmctl`/endpoint/supervisor shape. Host validation is green for the target `mvm-oci-init` zigbuild, `mvm-guest` tests, focused stale-cache `mvm-cli` tests, workspace check/clippy, and a live Alpine `ps aux` smoke. The checked baseline report remains open because the current host had unrelated HVF runtime processes and active build work; the attempted full density run failed at wave 1 with the substitution endpoint closing before ready, and cleanup left no matching Plan 237 processes behind.

**Additional 2026-07-09 — HVF OCI `--allow-host` availability gate fixed.** The macOS HVF OCI egress selector no longer treats the parent `mvmctl` process's hypervisor entitlement probe as the deciding signal for `{ no_guest_nic, host_vsock_proxy }`. The backend now advertises those capabilities only when the detached `mvm-hvf-supervisor` path is actually launchable on this host, and explicit `--hypervisor hvf` validation now refuses early when that helper path is unavailable. Result: a launchable supervisor is accepted for OCI `--allow-host` on macOS with no guest NIC, while unavailable helper paths still fail closed before OCI pull/boot work. Focused backend/CLI tests, macOS docs, and a packaged live proof script (`scripts/check-hvf-oci-allow-host-smoke.sh` / `just hvf-oci-allow-host-smoke`) were updated in the same change.

**Additional 2026-07-09 — Plan 241 guest vsock session helper refactor is COMPLETE on branch `feat/guest-vsock-session-refactor`.** The guest-side addon bridge and SOCKS5 egress client now share `mvm_guest_helpers::guest_vsock_session::HostVsockSession`, a small transport helper that owns only the repeated guest lifecycle: dial the host AF_VSOCK port, write already-framed initial bytes, then splice traffic bidirectionally. The addon peer-header prelude remains byte-for-byte identical, the SOCKS5 greeting/CONNECT flow and newline-terminated `host:port\n` target-line framing remain identical, and host-side claim-10 policy enforcement stays explicit and unchanged in the host crates. The same transport boundary now also carries a reserved first frame for absolute-form HTTP proxy requests, so BusyBox-style `GET https://… HTTP/1.1` traffic is still parsed, gated, and originated on the host side rather than in backend relay code. Coverage adds one shared-helper ordering witness plus guest-path regression tests for both call sites, along with host-side raw-egress and HTTP-forward dispatch tests. Validation is green with `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo test -p mvm-guest-helpers`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd raw_egress --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-hostd http_forward --lib`, `cargo clippy -p mvm-guest-helpers -p mvm-hostd --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo check --workspace`, `cargo test --workspace`, and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, all under isolated `MVM_DATA_DIR` / `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` / `CARGO_HOME` worktree env. A same-day live HVF proof on the rebased branch is now green too: after `mvmctl env sign` and a shorter `/tmp` data dir to avoid the substitution-endpoint UDS path-length limit, the required BusyBox HTTPS smoke passed with `exit:0`.

**Additional 2026-07-11 — Plan 245 backend endpoint relay trait is COMPLETE on branch `fix/host-http-forward-proxy`.** The HVF-side backend now depends on a transport-only `GuestEndpointRelay` trait at the per-VM endpoint byte-relay seam instead of concrete `SubstitutionBridge` method names. The trait covers guest bytes, endpoint drains, closes, and active-state checks only; host policy, raw egress, HTTP parsing, reqwest/TLS, and allowlist enforcement remain in `mvm-hostd`. The same slice also keeps source-checkout release builds off the embedded-helper zigbuild path by default while the explicit `release-artifact-bootstrap` feature still builds a self-contained shipped `mvmctl`. Validation is green with focused backend/guest/host/CLI tests, touched-crate clippy with `-D warnings`, the original `mvm-oci-init` `aarch64-unknown-linux-musl` zigbuild, `cargo run --release -- --version` without embedded zigbuild, and a production `release-artifact-bootstrap` `mvmctl` build using pinned Zig 0.13.0.

**Additional 2026-07-09 — Plan 238 streamed ext4 materialization + OCI/ext4 oracles COMPLETE.** `specs/plans/238-streamed-ext4-materialization-and-oracles.md` now tracks the shipped implementation, not just the proposal. `mvm-ext4` exposes streamed sparse-range emission (`emit_image[_with_options]`) with writer-level dense-vs-streamed byte-identity coverage, and the in-process `mvm-build` OCI/rootfs path now writes `rootfs.ext4` directly to the destination file instead of first building a dense `Vec<u8>` in memory. OCI-boundary differential coverage is green, and Linux-tool oracle tests (`e2fsck`, `debugfs`) passed on a Debian 6.1 host, including the corruption-rejection witness.
**Additional 2026-07-09 — Plan 240 vsock port-handler registry production-readiness follow-up is COMPLETE on branch `feat/vsock-port-handler-registry`.** The HVF-side vsock host-I/O loop no longer depends on the old fixed 5 ms backstop: the bridges expose their readiness-relevant fds, the I/O thread maintains a dynamic `mio::Poll` registration set for agent/console listeners plus established host streams and relay sockets, and topology changes wake the thread so it can resync instead of polling. Focused validation is green (`cargo test -p mvm-backend vsock --lib`, `cargo clippy -p mvm-backend --lib --tests -- -D warnings`), and the live libkrun backend boot remains green with a freshly built local `mvm-libkrun-supervisor` (`cargo test -p mvm-backend --test libkrun_lifecycle_e2e -- --ignored --nocapture` under `MVM_LIBKRUN_E2E=1`). The same branch removes the remaining active guest-NIC/gateway dependency from the libkrun workload and builder source path, replaces the Stage 0 SOCKS leg with the CONNECT-over-vsock proxy on `127.0.0.1:8443`, and leaves the live Hetzner host with no `gvproxy` or `passt` process in the path. Closeout evidence is now present too: after rebuilding `mvmctl`, a direct Hetzner capture confirms that `run --json --receipt` keeps stdout JSON-only while bridge / Firecracker / banner chrome routes to stderr, and the required post-fail-closed live Linux smoke is green with `MVM_LIVE_SMOKE=1 MVM_LIBKRUN_SUPERVISOR_PATH=/tmp/mvm-vsock-port-handler-registry-remote-target/debug/mvm-libkrun-supervisor CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/tmp/mvm-vsock-port-handler-registry-remote-target cargo test --test smoke_run_json_receipt -- --exact run_json_and_receipt_agree_without_raw_output --nocapture` (passed in 27.42 s). A same-day deletion slice finished the active-path rename too: `libkrun-sys` now uses a neutral `native_gateway` Rust surface and active scratch artifacts use `native-gateway.*`, so only compatibility-specific fixtures plus the upstream vfkit gateway-compat ABI entrypoint still mention `gvproxy`. That closes the remaining builder-path proof and the plan.
**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 builder/dev overlay attach slice LANDED in the same worktree.** The selected builder backends can now consume the cached runtime overlay as a distinct read-only filesystem instead of relying only on the baked builder rootfs copy. The HVF disk-transport builder spec now appends `mvm.runtime_source_policy=prefer_overlay` plus `mvm.runtime_data=/dev/vde`, attaches the cached overlay as a fifth read-only disk, and the builder PID 1 (`mvm-host-vm-init`) mounts that ext4 at `/mvm/runtime` read-only before the guest agent is forked. The libkrun steady-state builder paths now append the same prefer-overlay contract and attach the cached overlay as a read-only extra disk (`/dev/vdc`) for one-shot flake builds, shell jobs, and the persistent builder VM. Focused regressions now pin both sides of that implementation contract: `mvm-host-vm-init` tests the builder runtime-overlay mount flag bits and `mvm-build` tests that libkrun builder overlay attachments stay read-only and are skipped for root-dir images. The baked fallback intentionally remains in builder/dev images, and the plan still keeps any builder-tier `RequiredOverlay` flip plus live read-only proof open. Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-backend builder_runner --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-build --bin mvm-host-vm-init`, `cargo test -p mvm-build --lib`, `cargo check -p mvm-build -p mvm-backend --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-build -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-09 — Plan 239 next kernel-config subtraction pass COMPLETE.** The resolved builder/workload config inspection path is now first-class in the kernel flake, builder-vm flake, CLI docs, and kernel guide; the local `mvmctl build kernel build` path also now realizes a `workload-sizeopt` comparison build. The follow-up shared-base audit first proved `CONFIG_AUDIT` removable because the shipped audit posture is the userspace, chain-signed `host.audit.v1` flow rather than Linux kernel audit or `NETLINK_AUDIT`, then proved `CONFIG_BPF_JIT` removable because Linux 6.12 keeps the core interpreter with `CONFIG_BPF=y` and neither the sealed workload nor the builder path needs native-code BPF compilation. Refreshed `aarch64` metrics are now builder `1364` built-ins / `16,998,408` raw / `7,562,916` gzip; workload `1320` / `15,796,232` / `7,010,163`; workload-sizeopt `1320` / `13,881,352` / `5,951,074`; and the workload budget gate is ratcheted to `1320`. The BPF follow-up is also conclusive: Linux 6.12's `CONFIG_NET` unconditionally selects `CONFIG_BPF`, and the workload cannot drop `NET` without losing the `AF_VSOCK` guest-agent/control-plane path plus admitted `--net` / `--allow-host` loopback TCP helpers and netlink route setup. `SECCOMP`, `SECCOMP_FILTER`, and `DM_VERITY` are not what keep `BPF` on, so the shipped decision is to keep `BPF_SYSCALL` off and retain core `CONFIG_BPF=y` until the workload networking contract changes. The synced tree's `mvm-kernel-config` `make defconfig` regression is fixed too: Kconfig's `$(shell,...)` helper needed a real `/bin/sh` inside the Nix sandbox, so the kernel recipe now provides it explicitly and fresh `--source compile --boot-check` workload witnesses are green again on the current tree.
**Additional 2026-07-09 — OCI `--image` cold-cache kernel acquisition corrected on branch `fix/oci-kernel-download`.** The prior source-checkout kernel fallback made a generic `machine run --image busybox ...` bootstrap Stage 0 and build the builder VM just to obtain a workload kernel. The resolver now keeps the production fast path first: reuse a dedicated/default cached kernel, reuse an already-built builder kernel for non-prod runs, otherwise download the versioned, hash-verified workload kernel. Stage 0 workload-kernel compilation remains available only through an explicit source-build request, so kernel developers can still exercise local `nix/images/kernel/*` edits without making ordinary OCI runs pay that cost. Docs now state this split directly.
**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 `libkrun` attach semantics ADVANCED in the same worktree.** The remaining block-backed backend now consumes the runtime overlay contract end-to-end instead of treating it as Firecracker/HVF/qemu-only metadata: the shared attach helper resolves cached overlays for `libkrun`, and the backend boots the verity initrd with a fixed read-only virtio-blk layout (`/dev/vda` rootfs, `/dev/vdb` verity, `/dev/vdc` runtime overlay, `/dev/vdd` runtime-overlay verity). `RequiredOverlay` `libkrun` boots now fail closed before supervisor spawn when verity metadata, an effective initrd, or the complete overlay artifact triple is missing. The remaining open work is intentional: the plan still keeps the `libkrun` policy flip and live read-only proof unchecked before any tier moves from `PreferOverlay` to `RequiredOverlay`. Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-backend libkrun --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-cli runtime_overlay_attach_tests --lib`, `cargo check -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 docs/update-model slice LANDED in the same worktree.** The public docs now match the overlay-required runtime contract instead of the older "guest agent is just baked into the rootfs" model. `reference/filesystem.md`, `reference/guest-agent.md`, `guides/nix-flakes.md`, and `reference/cli-commands.md` now describe the read-only `/mvm/runtime` overlay, the rootfs/runtime split for sealed images, the version-matched-not-latest invariant, and the supported update story: stopped VMs pick up a newer overlay on the next start/restart, while running VMs keep the version they booted with until restart. Validation in the worktree: `./node_modules/.bin/astro build` from `public/` completed successfully.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 audit-surface proof tightened in the same worktree.** The runtime-overlay rollout now has a command-layer proof that the typed boot contract actually reaches the chain-signed audit stream: `emit_boot_posture_audits_runtime_source_policy_label` drives real admission, emits `plan.boot_posture`, and asserts the resulting chain file records `runtime_source_policy="required-overlay"` from the enum label path. That closes the last gap between “the model carries the field” and “the observable audit surface is pinned by tests.” Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-cli emit_boot_posture_audits_runtime_source_policy_label --lib`, `cargo check -p mvm-cli --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 WSL2-first workload support ADVANCED, live host proof pending.** The WSL2-only implementation slice is now landed on the branch: `Platform::Wsl2` exposes a dedicated workload-host capability, `has_libkrun()` no longer hard-disables supported WSL2 shapes, backend auto-selection and CLI hypervisor auto-detect choose `libkrun` for WSL2 + nested `/dev/kvm` while keeping Firecracker off the WSL2 path, `mvmctl doctor` reports supported vs unsupported WSL2 states explicitly (including DrvFs repo/state refusals), and the Windows/WSL2 docs now describe the supported `libkrun` path instead of "future/experimental". `scripts/run-wsl2-libkrun-smoke.sh` is now a self-contained live-proof wrapper: existing libkrun backend lifecycle smoke + temporary HTTP scaffold/build + transient JSON/receipt smoke + guest-agent/exec + `--allow-host` egress + `machine forward` + clean stop. Local validation is green (`cargo test -p mvm-core platform`, `cargo test -p mvm-backend auto_select_kind`, `cargo test -p mvm-cli auto_detect_hypervisor`, `cargo test -p mvm-cli doctor`, `cargo check --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`). Remaining before the plan can be marked complete: execute the WSL2 smoke on a real supported WSL2 host and record that proof.
**Additional 2026-07-09 — Plan 236 proposed: host-authority runtime roadmap.** A new integration plan now ties together the current transport, host-services, client-facade, rootfs, secret-egress, and vsock-only networking work into one execution line. The plan's priority order is explicit: first finish the host-authority boundary and real no-guest-NIC vsock-only data plane; then ship a narrow explicit secret-egress feature and caller-owned runtime lifecycle; then tighten runtime-share/filesystem semantics and publish stronger operational evidence. It explicitly reuses already-active lanes instead of creating parallel epics: Plan 202, 204, 216, 219, 221, 227, and 230, plus the active worktrees for agent-verb grant delivery, the vsock port-handler registry, and vsock-only egress cutover. The broad transparent-network branch is recorded as exploratory input, not the architecture baseline.

The three remaining stale prerequisite branches were then refreshed into clean Codex worktrees — `codex/plan-236-plan202-refresh`, `codex/plan-236-vsock-egress-refresh`, and `codex/plan-236-plan216-refresh` — and all three rebase cleanly and collapse to `origin/main`. That clears the stale-branch blocker; remaining Plan 236 blockers are now the honest live-proof / production-evidence gaps rather than prerequisite branch hygiene.
**Additional 2026-07-10 — Plan 236 production OCI verity-seal fallback implemented on branch `feat/plan-236-oci-seal-closeout`.** The OCI `--prod` run path no longer stops at the old non-Linux `HostUnsupported` verity gap: `crates/mvm-build/src/run_image.rs` now routes dm-verity sealing through the existing builder-shell-job boundary when local `veritysetup` support is unavailable, preserves the all-or-nothing initrd+sidecar semantics, and emits the pinned `veritysetup format` script shape the builder VM already carries. Coverage adds direct fallback-path tests (local success stays local, `HostUnsupported` falls back, real local errors still surface) plus script-shape and rollback witnesses. Validation is green on the branch with focused `mvm-build` tests, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test --workspace` on the host. The remaining blocker is now live production proof of that new path, not code-path absence.
**Additional 2026-07-11 — Plan 236 OCI prod closeout now has a reviewer-ready live-smoke witness plus an arm64 OCI compatibility fix.** The same `feat/plan-236-oci-seal-closeout` branch now carries the missing disabled-by-default macOS witness for `mvmctl run --image --prod`: `tests/oci_image_runner_smoke.rs` still requires an explicit signed digest-pinned `MVM_OCI_IMAGE_RUNNER_PROD_REF`, but it can synthesize a temporary Chainguard OCI policy when `MVM_OCI_POLICY` is absent. While driving the live proof, the branch also fixed a real production OCI bug in `mvm-oci`: `linux/arm64/v8` hosts now accept valid `linux/arm64` image-index entries that omit the optional variant field. Focused validation is green with `cargo test --test oci_image_runner_smoke -- --nocapture`, `cargo clippy --test oci_image_runner_smoke -- -D warnings`, targeted `mvm-oci` tests, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-oci --all-targets -- -D warnings`. The blocker is now purely environment/evidence: a suitable signed digest-pinned witness image still needs to be executed end to end on a host with working microVM runtime prerequisites.
**Additional 2026-07-11 — Plan 236 OCI prod closeout hardened the remaining pre-boot compatibility gaps on the same branch.** The live witness then exposed three more real issues and the branch fixed all three without widening the architecture: `mvm-oci` now accepts the standard pseudo-device nodes OCI images carry on non-Linux hosts by recording them as skipped rather than refusing the layer; the pure ext4 collector in `mvm-build` can temporarily read owner-unreadable files such as `/etc/shadow` and restore the exact guest-visible mode afterward; and the libkrun builder shell-job path now applies the same disconnected/vsock-only networking transform as the normal builder path, so dm-verity sealing no longer boots an accidental guest-NIC config. Focused validation is green with targeted `mvm-oci` tests, `cargo test -p mvm-build collect_nodes_reads_owner_unreadable_files_without_changing_guest_mode --features pure-mkfs`, `cargo test -p mvm-build --lib builder_shell_krun_context_keeps_shell_jobs_nicless --features 'builder-vm pure-mkfs'`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-build --lib --tests --features pure-mkfs -- -D warnings`. The remaining prod witness failure is now external release readiness rather than a runtime bug: the hash-verified workload-kernel download refuses to proceed because `v0.17.0` is still missing `kernel-aarch64-checksums-sha256.txt` on GitHub Releases.
**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 checkpoint metadata now pins runtime-overlay identity in the same worktree.** Checkpoint records now carry the boot-time runtime contract (`runtime_source_policy` plus optional `runtime_overlay_version`), both capture paths preserve that metadata, and child checkpoint lineage inherits it. The fs_quick `checkpoint fork --boot` seam now prefers the recorded overlay version when one exists instead of silently rebinding to whatever overlay version is newest in cache; a malformed `RequiredOverlay` checkpoint with no recorded version is refused before boot, while older checkpoints without the new fields fall back to the derived policy for compatibility. `vm restore` still returns a tracked-unavailable error on current backends, so there is still no restore path that can drift to an unrelated overlay version. Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-core checkpoint --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-backend checkpoint --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-cli checkpoint --lib`, `cargo check -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 overlay-required runtime rollout ADVANCED into Phase C in the worktree.** Sealed `mkGuest` roots now derive `runtimeLean = isSealed` and omit the baked `/usr/local/bin/mvm-guest-agent` + `mvm-guest-netinit` fallback block, while dev-tier images keep the fallback until every local backend can attach the overlay. The `mvm-meta.json` sidecar now records `runtimeLean`, and runtime-overlay admission was tightened from a pure `overlayAware` check to a policy-aware gate: `RequiredOverlay` boots must carry `runtimeLean: true`, but `PreferOverlay` / `RootfsOnly` preserve the old sidecar compatibility path. Firecracker, qemu, and libkrun now pass the resolved `runtime_source_policy` into that admission helper. Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-build builder_vm --lib`, `cargo test --test nix_flake_structure`, `cargo check -p mvm-build -p mvm-backend --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-build -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`. Remaining Plan 242 work stays open for the rest of Phase D/E, especially libkrun attach, builder/dev attach, and the explicit stopped-vs-running overlay update model.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 lifecycle metadata ADVANCED in the same worktree.** The runtime-overlay rollout now records the concrete booted overlay contract in backend-neutral VM state: `VmStartConfig` carries `runtime_overlay_version`, the host-side attach helper stores the resolver's actual artifact version, and `runtime_meta::record_from_start_config` writes both `runtime_source_policy` and `runtime_overlay_version` into `~/.mvm/vms/<name>/mode.json` alongside `rootfs_path`. That gives later checkpoint/restore and cold-update work one source of truth for what a VM really booted with instead of inferring from the mutable cache directory. Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-backend runtime_meta --lib`, `cargo test -p mvm-cli runtime_overlay_attach_tests --lib`, `cargo check -p mvm-backend -p mvm-cli --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-backend -p mvm-cli --all-targets -- -D warnings`. Remaining E3 work is still open: snapshot/restore must either require the same overlay version or refuse until a compatibility policy exists.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 242 fs_quick fork boot path now honors the runtime-overlay contract.** The child-boot seam in `checkpoint fork --boot` no longer defaults to `rootfs_only`: it derives `runtime_source_policy` from the forked child rootfs plus selected backend and reuses the same overlay-attach helper ordinary workload boots use before `backend.start()`. As a result, a sealed Firecracker fs_quick child now selects `RequiredOverlay` and fails closed on a missing overlay instead of silently booting a rootfs-only fallback because it came from a checkpoint fork. Validation in the worktree: `cargo test -p mvm-cli checkpoint --lib`, `cargo check -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend --all-targets`, and `cargo clippy -p mvm-cli -p mvm-backend --all-targets -- -D warnings`. Remaining lifecycle work is still the hard part: resumed/restored VMs need an explicit same-version-or-refuse rule rather than just this fresh-boot enforcement.

**Additional 2026-07-09 — Plan 242 overlay-required runtime rollout ADVANCED again in the worktree.** The earlier contract, host-side fail-closed, guest policy, selector-helper, transient attach, HVF attach, and qemu attach slices remain landed. The Firecracker B3 proof lane is now complete on the real host `88.99.197.234`: a purpose-built minimal verity guest under `/root/overlay-proof-manual/20260709-015530-minimal-proof/` recorded `/mvm/runtime` mounted as `ext4 ro` and both `touch` plus overwrite attempts failing with `Read-only file system`; a tampered runtime-roothash boot under `/root/overlay-proof-manual/20260709-015911-tamper-proof/` died inside `mvm-verity-init` before `/init` with `metadata block 1 is corrupted`; and the overlay-agent proof boot under `/root/overlay-proof-manual/20260709-020906-agent-proof/` reached `mvm-verity-init: switching to /init` then panicked with `PID: 1 Comm: agent`, proving PID 1 had already exec'd the runtime-overlay agent. The host-side cold-cache refusal path is also pinned on the same machine by the exact `mvm-cli` attach-helper test
`commands::vm::up::runtime_overlay_attach_tests::firecracker_cold_cache_errors_when_overlay_is_required`, which passes only when an empty runtime-overlay cache errors before launch instead of falling back. Remaining work stays open in the plan for the rootfs/runtime split itself (Phase C onward) plus libkrun and builder/dev follow-ons.

**Additional 2026-07-08 — Plan 213 §I builder-pack revocation consumer LANDED on branch `feat/plan-213-revocation-net`.** Installed builder-pack resolution now refreshes a project-signed `pack-revocations.json`/`.bundle` from the `revocations` release, caches it under `~/.cache/mvm/pack-revocations/` with a 24-hour refresh / 7-day offline tolerance / `404` bootstrap rule, and unions any fetched entries with the operator's local `pack-trust.json` revocations. Verification failures on the fetched list never apply the public channel; enforcement falls back to the on-disk config only. Coverage: focused absent-cache + garbage-bundle tests in `mvm-cli`, plus the green PR CI matrix. Producer/publish remains tracked separately.
**Additional 2026-07-08 — OCI `--image` NIC-less vsock-egress activation landed in the macOS cutover lane.** The runtime-injected guest `/init` now includes and launches `mvm-egress-client`, exports SOCKS proxy env vars, and the embedded/cacheable guest-runtime pipeline carries the third guest binary end-to-end for OCI `--image` runs. The final activation is now tied to the **requested outbound policy** (`--net` / `--allow-host`), not a hidden opt-in env var: the OCI exec/preflight/receipt path injects and records proxy env vars automatically, and backend selection now requires a backend that honestly advertises `{ vsock, no_guest_nic, host_vsock_proxy }`. Today that means `hvf` and `vz`; incapable backends are refused up front instead of silently degrading to a guest NIC. The same gate now applies to persistent OCI-backed machine boots (`machine run -d --image ...` and `machine start` on an image-backed machine), so the stored lifecycle can no longer fall back to the legacy NIC/gateway path after a safe transient preflight. Vz now omits the guest NIC/gvproxy entirely on this raw OCI egress path and spawns the per-VM host egress endpoint before boot so the guest fails closed if the endpoint is absent. Scope is intentionally TCP/HTTP-only: ICMP is still unsupported, so `ping` remains an invalid smoke for `--allow-host`. This advances Plan 226 WS-N / Plan 227 WS-B activation plumbing but does not yet flip every backend to vsock-only egress or delete gvproxy/passt globally.

**Additional 2026-07-07 — Plan 213 Slice 2 (release signing custody) DESIGNED, docs-only.** Settled the custody model for the two Slice-1 deferrals (embedded release-trust default + release-pipeline wiring) and recorded it as an ADR-097 §9 in-place amendment + a Slice 2 section in `specs/plans/213-…md`. Decision: **keyless** signing for the public mvm release channel (GitHub Actions OIDC → Fulcio → Rekor; no long-lived key; detached Sigstore bundle sidecar over the manifest bytes), verified against embedded Fulcio/Rekor roots + a compiled-in **identity allow-list** (issuer + subject-pattern pinned to the release workflow on a protected tag; list, not scalar, so identity migration needs no key-overlap window) + the Rekor inclusion proof; the ed25519 `pack-trust.json` path is **unchanged** as the operator/enterprise/fleet lane and stays purely additive on top of the always-on embedded root. Structure (Option Y): a `manifest-verify`-gated keyless verifier in `mvm-core` reusing the *same* shared structural/hash/policy/expiry/revocation middle as `verify_pack_at` (only the signature-key step differs), trust inputs as parameters — so mvmd reaches it with one feature flip and layers fleet policy on top. Revocation stays config-driven this slice; live-channel fetch + disable/pin-to-operator switch deferred to §I. Four units (keyless verifier + manifest authority shape; embedded allow-list + un-inert the installed path; release-pipeline produce/sign/publish; network-fetch completion step). **No code changed; docs only, branch `worktree-plan-213-release-signing-custody`.**

**Additional 2026-07-07 — #1527 HVF kernel-image sharing MEASURED + issue triage pruned.** Raw-HVF kernel boot now uses anonymous demand-zero guest RAM plus a `MAP_PRIVATE | MAP_FIXED` mapping of the configured kernel file over the kernel load window, so the supervisor no longer copies `vmlinux` into each VM's private anonymous RAM. Live macOS-26 Apple Silicon measurement with a temporary guest-agent initramfs: baseline `main` supervisor `phys_footprint` 569.6 MiB for one / 1,139.2 MiB for two; file-backed branch 40.9 MiB for one / 81.9 MiB for two, with `vmmap` showing the exact `vmlinux` path as a private-COW file mapping. The change stays HVF-only and does not add Vz, gvproxy, TAP, bridge, guest NIC, guest IP, or Firecracker networking behavior. Issue cleanup closed stale #1227 (builderd routing/raw-shell gate already merged; future proof must be fresh HVF/vsock-only validation) and completed #1388 (public machine/client boot, list, persistence seams present); remaining old open issues were kept only when still useful under the no-guest-NIC/vsock-only direction.

**Additional 2026-07-06 — Plan 213 Slice 1 (attested fast-first-boot pack) — Units 1–3 LANDED on branch `worktree-plan213-slice1-builder-pack`, with a mid-slice target correction.** Three thin units, each TDD + two-stage-reviewed, all gates green: **Unit 1** `mvm-core::packs::PackBuilder` (signed-manifest producer, inverse of `verify_pack_at`) + `PackBackend::Hvf`; **Unit 2** `mvm-core::pack_cache` (content-addressed verify + quarantine→atomic-promote, `resolve_pack`, fail-closed re-verify on read); **Unit 3** the `MVM_BUILDER_PACK`-gated **attested builder-image download materializer** in `mvm-cli::dev_vz` (places a verified Builder pack's `vmlinux`+`rootfs.ext4` into `BUILDER_DIR/<arch>` so the installed acquisition path takes `UseCached`; fail-open to the plain checksum download). **Correction (recon + isolated live probe):** the original "seed the Stage-0 nix store" target was retired — Stage 0 runs only on source checkouts (which the pack path excludes), while installed binaries take `DownloadPublished` and get their base `/nix/store` from the `rootfs.ext4` overlay lowerdir; readiness on the installed path is `validate_builder_vm_stage0_artifacts` (placed artifacts), not the source-checkout fingerprint marker. See `specs/plans/213-…md` §"Slice 1" + §"Slice 1 correction".

**Additional 2026-07-06 — `MvmClient` facade consolidated: trait folded into `mvm-core`, two client crates → one.** The `MvmClient` trait + DTOs + `MockBackend` + remote `GatewayBackend` moved from the standalone `mvm-client` (leaf) crate into `mvm-core::client`, behind a `client` feature (gateway behind `client-remote`); `async-trait` is a proc-macro so `mvm-core`'s default closure stays `tokio`-free (`check-core-runtime-free` green) and the guest never enables the feature (`check-guest-agent-runtime-free` green). The former `mvm-client-local` (`LocalBackend`) was renamed to **`mvm-client`** — now the single user-facing crate, re-exporting the `mvm-core::client` surface so consumers write one import (`use mvm_client::{MvmClient, MachineSpec, LocalBackend};`) instead of two. `connect(Target::Local)` now constructs `LocalBackend` directly. `mvm-sdk`'s `client-facade` enables `mvm-core/client` (not a separate crate); the cycle guard `no_backend_dep.rs` is preserved (asserts default+facade builds link neither `mvm-backend` nor the heavy `mvm-client`). Supersedes the ADR-105 two-crate split (that ADR's "rejected: put it in `mvm-core`" bullet was wrong — the runtime-free gate forbids only `tokio`, not `async-trait`). Also fixed a latent break: `SubprocessBackend` was missing `reconfigure_machine` (the facade only builds under `client-facade`, which no CI lane compiled). Tests: mvm-core `--features client-remote` (1542), mvm-client default/remote (15/17), mvm-sdk `--features client-facade` (370) all green. CI: add a `cargo nextest run -p mvm-core --features client-remote` lane (mirrors the `hostd-transport` lane) so the moved unit tests stay gated.

**Additional 2026-07-06 — internal `mvm-cli` modularity cleanup prepared for merge.** The top-level CLI startup path no longer keeps its execution match inline: `crates/mvm-cli/src/commands/mod.rs` is reduced to small startup helpers plus a trait-backed dispatcher in `crates/mvm-cli/src/commands/dispatch.rs`, so early helper commands (`__qemu-vsock-bridge`, `__ssh-agent-proxy`) and normal dispatch are separated cleanly. `crates/mvm-cli/src/commands/image/mod.rs` also shed the OCI registry-auth / cosign trust helpers into `crates/mvm-cli/src/commands/image/trust.rs`, bringing the production portion of `image/mod.rs` back under the 1500-line target once its inline tests are excluded. **Additional 2026-07-05 — Plan 219 (agent verb grant delivery) Firecracker proof PARTIAL.** Issue #1381 follow-on is wired for the non-live seams and now has Linux/KVM Firecracker proof for the load-bearing grant path: a restricted boot with `agent_verbs=["ping","run-entrypoint"]` returned `Ping -> Pong` and unlisted `UpdateIdleTimeout -> VerbNotAuthorized`, while a true no-grant dev-profile boot serialized no `agent_verbs`, wrote no `verb-grant.json`, and returned `UpdateIdleTimeoutAck`. The remaining gvproxy/passt gateway code (libkrun/Vz/Firecracker networking) stays in place for now because it is still load-bearing — its removal is staged behind the host-vsock-proxy replacement (the HVF virtio-vsock work on `feat/plan-214-hvf-spike`); deleting it before the replacement lands would strip networking from the working backends.

The macOS backend direction is ADR-098 (HVF as the macOS backend, Vz sunset). Implementation is underway as stacked/independent PRs: #1336 capability model → #1337 Machine lib → #1338 fail-closed selection → #1339 select_backend → #1340 →VmStartConfig; #1342 snapshot frame v0 (Phase 8); #1341 fixes a pre-existing nondeterministic `check-stubs` Lint gate that was failing all PRs. Key finding: mvm already implements most of the target (backend trait + capabilities, warm-standby pool, chain-signed audit, vsock secret substitution + redaction, static PID-1 inits, signed plans, OCI+Nix pipelines, compile-to-IR SDKs, SSH ban) — the work is consolidation + completion, not a rewrite. The named gaps: a product `Machine` library (orchestration lives in the CLI today), `NetworkMode::{None default, HostVsockProxy}` with host egress/ingress brokers + guest `mvm-netd` replacing the NIC-by-default path, an extended `BackendCapabilities` model, one consolidated static `mvm-init` with lifecycle markers + inspectable failure + `snapshot_at`, a cap/bounds-checked snapshot frame v0, eager-CoW `MAP_PRIVATE` local restore (spiked before userfaultfd is made the foundation), a `ResidentMemoryAccountant` separate from the spawn-concurrency gate, end-to-end `trace_id`/`span_id` audit correlation, and plan-recorded input-kind + all artifact digests. Coordinates with Plans 207/208/211/212/213 and ADR-002. **No code changed; docs only, on branch `chore/architecture-clean-replacement-review`.** Plan 214 is Proposed and not yet scheduled.

**Additional 2026-06-22 — Plan 204 WS-D build-default flip + raw-shell debug gate MERGED (#1250); supersedes the two 2026-06-21 "merge held / deferred" entries below.** The guest-image build route now defaults on: `try_typed_build` reads `build_typed_opt_in`, so a reachable daemon is used unless `MVM_BUILDERD_RAW_SHELL` forces the legacy in-VM shell build; flake check stays opt-in via `MVM_BUILDERD_TYPED`. The full WS-D typed-routing chain is merged — adapter seam + diagnostic + `check-builder-shell-job-sites` lint, typed `FlakeCheck` from `mvmctl build validate` (#1201), typed `BuildGuestImage` from `dev_build` with host-readable `/out` export (#1211/#1213/#1215), daemon nix-env fix (#1226), default-flip + raw-shell gate (#1250). The `check-builder-shell-job-sites` allowlist stays at 4 (the `persistent_builder.rs` legacy construction is retained as the daemon-unreachable + raw-shell fallback, so it legitimately stays allowlisted). **The one remaining item is a verification, not a routing blocker:** the live per-backend artifact-equality proof (`store_path` typed == in-VM `nix build … --print-out-paths`, libkrun + Vz on macOS, Firecracker on KVM) — boot-gated, tracked as Plan 204 Verification §"typed guest image build".

**Additional 2026-06-21 — Plan 204 WS-D build-default flip + raw-shell debug gate implemented (branch `feat/plan-204-wsd-flip-build-default`; merge held for a clean equality proof).** `try_typed_build` now reads a new `build_typed_opt_in` (typed whenever a daemon is reachable, unless `MVM_BUILDERD_RAW_SHELL` forces the legacy in-VM shell build); `try_typed_flake_check` is untouched, so the flip is **build-only** (flake check stays opt-in via `MVM_BUILDERD_TYPED`). 3 new unit tests (default-on + gate semantics + build↔flake-check independence); fmt + `check-builder-shell-job-sites` (stays 4 — the `persistent_builder.rs` legacy construction is retained as the daemon-unreachable + raw-shell fallback) + `check-no-host-nix` + `check-no-spec-refs-in-comments` clean; full-workspace clippy `-D warnings` clean under a single rustup-stable toolchain. **Routing behaviour live-confirmed on macOS-26 Vz** (typed attempted by default with the gate unset; straight-to-legacy under `MVM_BUILDERD_RAW_SHELL=1` — both directions in one run). **The merge gate stays open: the literal artifact-equality proof.** A live run on this loaded box hit exactly the documented cascade (stale-resident-builder `/work`-not-a-flake, missing `mvm-vz-supervisor` bin, stale dispatch socket, cold-cache Stage 0 `BadActivate`) — none of it WS-D code — so the default-flip stays on the branch until a clean per-backend equality proof (`store_path` typed == in-VM `nix build … --print-out-paths`, same target both paths) is green; the parallel libkrun + Firecracker proofs cover the other two backends.

**Additional 2026-06-21 — Plan 204 WS-D typed `BuildGuestImage` dev_build route landed (opt-in, default-off); live proof caught + fixed a daemon nix-env bug; green proof + flip deferred.** #1213 (host-readable `output_dir` export to the `/out` share) + #1215 (`dev_build_via_builder_vm_uncached` wiring behind `MVM_BUILDERD_TYPED`) merged. The override-input gap needs no wire change — the typed route is reached only from the live-persistent-session branch, and that persistent legacy path (`persistent_builder::stage_flake_dispatch_job`) builds `nix build /work#<attr>` override-free (drops `mounts.staged_user_flake`; the source-checkout `--override-input mvm` lives only in the single-shot path), so typed-persistent and shell-persistent are override-free-equal by construction. **A live typed `BuildGuestImage` against a freshly-rebaked vz builder (macOS-26) caught a real bug:** the daemon spawned `nix` with no environment, so `HOME` was unset (`//.cache`) and `nix` died "creating directory '//.cache/nix': Read-only file system" — the typed path could not build at all (so was not artifact-equal). Fixed: `builderd::CommandExecutor` now applies the same writable `HOME=/tmp` + `XDG_CACHE_HOME=/nix-store/.cache` + `XDG_STATE_HOME=/tmp/.local/state` the shell build script exports, and creates the cache/state dirs first (`daemon_command_env`, unit-tested); plus a `builderd-buildimage` diagnostic that drives one typed build over the live socket. **Deferred to a quiet box** (blocked by the isolated-cold `dev up` environment on a loaded box — cascade of vz-supervisor-missing / stale-embed / parked-state / extraction-hang, none of them WS-D code): the literal over-the-wire artifact-equality proof (`store_path` typed == in-VM `nix build`, on a target without a `git+file:///work` input — a git *worktree*'s `.git` is a pointer file the builder VM can't resolve), then the build-specific `resolve_route` default-flip (`try_typed_build` defaults Typed when reachable + a debug opt-out for legacy), then dropping the routed file from the `check-builder-shell-job-sites` allowlist.

**Additional 2026-06-21 — Plan 200 OCI `machine run --net` egress now works end-to-end (Vz); DNS/L4 scan-composition fixed.** Final layer of the `--net` chain (after the supervisor egress-bundle fix and the guest-network-bringup fix). **Supersedes the earlier "gateway/egress-bridge does not forward" note — that diagnosis was wrong.** Instrumenting the vz splice showed the bridge forwards correctly (ARP resolves the gateway; gvproxy replies); the real blocker was the **egress scan**: under a curated allow-list (the `--net` dev preset = TCP/443 to specific hosts), `L4PolicyScan` dropped the guest's DNS query (UDP/53 to the gateway resolver) because the resolver isn't a TCP/443 allow-list entry, and it ran *before* `DnsSinkholeScan` (the intended DNS authority) — so **no hostname resolved at all**, even allow-listed ones. Fix (`mvm-hostd::supervisor::network::stages`): `L4PolicyScan` now **defers egress UDP/53 to the DNS layer** (symmetric with `DnsSinkholeScan`, which inspects only UDP/53), and `build_egress_scan` **always installs `DnsSinkholeScan` when L4 is a restrictive `Rules`** (empty allow-list ⇒ sink-hole every lookup) so deferring DNS can't leave it open on an IP-only policy. Deny-all is unaffected — its egress flow never opens at the coarse `PlanFlowPolicy` gate; `Unrestricted`/open keeps DNS open. 5 new unit tests; backend-agnostic (also covers libkrun). **Live-proven on macOS-26 Vz:** `machine run --net --image alpine` now resolves + connects allow-listed hosts (`nslookup github.com` → `140.82.113.4`; `wget https://api.github.com/zen` → "Approachable is better than simple.") while a non-allow-listed lookup is correctly sink-holed (`nslookup example.com` times out). The three `--net` fixes (egress-bundle resolution, guest-net bringup, DNS/L4 composition) together make OCI `--net` functional on the Vz transient path.

**Additional 2026-06-21 — Plan 200 OCI `machine run --net` guest-network bringup fixed (Vz):** second layer of the `--net` chain, after the supervisor egress-bundle fix. The in-guest netinit (`mvm_guest::guest_net::configure_guest_network`) shelled out to `/bin/udhcpc` and hard-errored before the static fallback when that binary was absent — which it is in a minimal OCI rootfs (alpine) — so the guest came up with eth0 link-up but no address, no default route, and no `/etc/resolv.conf` ("Network unreachable"). It also bind-mounted `/etc/resolv.conf` via `/bin/busybox`, which fails when the rootfs has no such target. Fixed both to degrade to the in-process paths: a `udhcpc` spawn failure is now treated as "no lease" so `configure_static` (ioctls, no in-guest tools) runs, and `seed_resolv_conf` falls back to a direct write. Live-proven on macOS-26 Vz: eth0 now gets `192.168.127.2/24`, `default via 192.168.127.1`, and `nameserver 192.168.127.1`; the console shows the static-fallback path firing. The third layer (DNS/L4 scan composition) is fixed separately — see the note above; together the three `--net` fixes make OCI `--net` functional on the Vz transient path.

Plan 189 WS-1 `vm save`/`vm restore` aliases landed (#1118). Plan 205 (resident builder control plane + residency model) is complete: WS-A/B/D/F merged (#1090/#1094/#1099/#1103), WS-E via #1102, WS-C's resident builder daemon delivered by Plan 204's `mvm-builderd` (#1091), the builder-tier trust gate (#1110) machine-checks all three tiers (`clean (3 rows)`), `MVM_RESIDENCY` and `mvmctl doctor` residency reporting are wired, Vz dev-builder park/restore + auto-park + invocation keeper are implemented, and the live macOS-26 Vz closeout runner passed in `/tmp/mvm-plan205-live-proof9` with warm reuse 130 ms, parked restore P50 643 ms / P95 1163 ms, zero command failures, final state `parked`, and live OCI `run --image docker.io/library/alpine:3.20 -- /bin/true` exit 0. FC live-memory remains delegated to Plan 175 and resident-daemon lifecycle refinements remain delegated to Plan 204; they no longer gate Plan 205. The builder-residency decision core carries `BuilderResidencyAction` plus builder snapshot freshness checks in `mvm-core::residency` (#1121). One correction from execution: workload-standby freshness is the existing `StandbyCompat` (kernel+image sha), not the builder fingerprint (Plan 195's fingerprint is builder-VM scope). The `host_signer` trust-gate false-positive is closed (#1123): builder/prod-agent gates now narrow their checks to the right authority-bearing crates, and persistent OCI machines document `warm_pool_size = 0` as intentional because named machines do not use the warm pool. Plan 193 transparent terminator wiring is explicitly blocked on rvproxy schema/support, not an mvm-only code slice: the local rvproxy config and `GatewayConfig` surfaces have no typed transparent `:80`/`:443` interception field or host-terminator destination, so mvm must keep libkrun/Vz at `VsockUdsChannel` and must not claim `supports_transparent_terminator()` until rvproxy lands the contract. Remaining Plan 193 work is rvproxy transparent-terminator contract/implementation, mvm wiring once that exists, then splice/Plan-141 `on_packet` deletion. Plan 202 is complete. Plan 200 portable-artifact preview now has a reusable verified-admission gate: `machine check-artifact` derives admission only after `.mvm` signature/hash/format verification and host-arch acceptance, with machine-level wrong-key, tamper, and arch-mismatch refusal tests. Plan 200 SDK artifact-verification parity now exposes that CLI-owned `machine check-artifact` path through Python `Machine.check_artifact`, TypeScript `Machine.checkArtifact`, and Rust `MachineCheckArtifact`; shared-fixture/fake-CLI tests prove SDKs route to `mvmctl machine ...` instead of privately verifying `.mvm` artifacts. Plan 200 docs/source guards now cover the beginner machine story with scenario-led use cases, explicit limitations, and a CI-wired `xtask check-machine-doc-guards` gate against implying host Nix, GPU, ICMP, or unsupported architectures are default-supported. Remaining Plan 200 C2/auth proof is live admission/non-bypass proof and live VM SSH-agent round-trip after rerunning the Firecracker in-guest raw probe through the port-5301 runtime-UDS fix; remaining portable-artifact product work is live `machine run <artifact>` / `machine pack` plus workflow docs. Plan 159 is closed: VZ/macOS scope is shipped, verb/install/product polish is rehomed to Plan 181 / Plan 200, and signed delta-image distribution is descoped until an owning artifact/distribution plan is opened.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 204 over-the-wire `FlakeCheck` transport live-proven + daemon experimental-features bug fixed:** a new diagnostic example `crates/mvm-build/examples/builderd-flakecheck.rs` (the first `BuilderdClient` consumer — a single-operation harness, not WS-D build-routing) drove a real `FlakeCheck` over the live `vsock-21473.sock` of the running macOS-26 Vz persistent builder: connect → handshake protocol v1 → `FlakeCheck` request → typed `Failed{NixEval}` terminal, so request → in-VM `nix` exec → classification → typed terminal round-trip end-to-end with no hang. The live run caught a real daemon bug the fake-executor unit tests could not: the builder image's `nix.conf` does not enable the `nix-command`/`flakes` experimental features, so every daemon `nix` call failed `error: experimental Nix feature 'nix-command' is disabled`. **Fix:** the daemon now passes `--extra-experimental-features "nix-command flakes"` explicitly on all four Nix argvs (`flake_check_argv`/`nix_build_argv`/`prefetch_source_argv`/`query_store_path_argv`, via a shared `nix_argv` helper) instead of depending on the image's global config; argv unit tests updated + a `nix_argv` test added (75 builderd tests green, fmt/clippy/`check-no-spec-refs-in-comments` clean). **Plan 204 still stays open** — the remaining over-the-wire proof is a clean `Completed`, which needs this fix carried into a rebuilt builder image *and* a valid flake staged into a builder-VM-visible path (the WS-D source-staging work); the fix is unit-verified but not yet re-proven in a running daemon (the daemon is baked into the rootfs, so re-proof needs an image rebuild). The glance box stays `[ ]`.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 206 on-host slices landed (UFFD + live-KVM tail remains):** the two Plan-206 pieces verifiable without a live KVM host are implemented and unit-tested; the macOS dev host cannot boot a KVM Firecracker VM, so every "proven on live KVM" acceptance line stays open. **Task 2 (primed barrier), host+guest wiring:** a new `GuestRequest::PrimedStatus` → `GuestResponse::PrimedStatusReport { primed }` RPC (mirrors `ProbeStatus`, wired through the whole verb/contract/profile surface, `deny_unknown_fields`); the workload asserts primed by creating `PRIMED_MARKER_PATH` (`/run/mvm/primed`, a no-privilege tmpfs write) and the agent reports presence via `workload_is_primed_at`; host-side `VsockPrimedSignalSource` polls the RPC over `vsock_transport::for_vm`, with the poll *policy* (`wait_for_primed_polling`) unit-tested against a fake "mock guest" and the per-poll vsock I/O left as the thin live-gated shell (mirrors `VsockPostRestoreSignal`); `mvmctl vm pause --primed-barrier [--primed-timeout=120]` calls `await_primed_barrier` before `pause_and_seal` and fails closed on timeout (no half-warmed snapshot; the hermetic `mock` hypervisor is never gated). **Task 3 (token-delivery polish):** S2 honest verb is done end to end — `post_restore_at` returns `PostRestoreReply { acknowledged, reseeded }`, `warm_restore_instance` returns a typed `ReseedStatus` (`Rotated`/`NotRotated`/`Undelivered`/`NotApplicable`, classified by the pure `classify_reseed`), `VmBackend::warm_start` returns `WarmStartOutcome { id, reseed }` (libkrun disk-only = `NotApplicable`), and `run_warm_start` prints `reseed.resume_summary()` so the line reflects the real outcome instead of unconditionally claiming "VMGenID rotated"; S1's fallback widens the post-resume agent-ready wait 30s→60s with a budget regression test. Remaining Plan 206 work is **Task 1** (the UFFD/NBD/hugepages substrate — Linux-kernel + live-KVM, unverifiable on a macOS dev host) and the three live-KVM acceptance proofs (T2-S3 seal-on-signal, T3-S1 root-cause of the ~30s latency, T3-S3 live reseed divergence). All on-host layers green: fmt + workspace clippy clean, doctests pass, `xtask check-no-spec-refs-in-comments` clean, ~30 new/related unit tests across `mvm-core`/`mvm-guest`/`mvm-backend`/`mvm`/`mvm-cli`.

**Additional 2026-06-19 — Plan 205 "instant" bar made a CI/live gate:** Plan 205's residency acceptance was hardened from "measured, not asserted" into an explicit latency budget (new "Latency budget" section). The PR matrix gates the deterministic no-boot invariant; the backend-bearing lane gates end-to-end `mvmctl` behavior on real hardware. The closeout gate is warm reuse under 250 ms and parked Vz restore P50 under 800 ms with P95 ≤ 2× P50. The earlier 100 ms figure remains the raw saved-state aspiration, not the shipping end-to-end CLI/supervisor/gvproxy budget. The first-ever image-download cost is explicitly out of the budget — paid once at install/prefetch time (`mvmctl bootstrap` prefetch / install-script prefetch), never on the per-command hot path.

**Additional 2026-06-21 — Plan 193 libkrun transparent terminator wiring:** the previous rvproxy-schema blocker is lifted for libkrun. rvproxy now exposes typed transparent `:80`/`:443` interception to a host terminator, and mvm wires libkrun native rvproxy config to that terminator: `[transparent]` is rendered, the libkrun supervisor carries the per-VM loopback port, and the substitution endpoint accepts rvproxy's original-destination preamble. Vz remains guarded at `VsockUdsChannel` until its launch path emits and uses the same native rvproxy config. Remaining Plan 193 work is Vz wiring, the live allow/deny matrix, then splice/Plan-141 hook deletion.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 parked-resume budget reconciled (#1141):** the parked (`min=0`) resume budget is not a hard `< 100 ms` full-memory-restore gate. Parked trades resume latency for zero idle RAM; the correctness bar is **no slower than a cold boot of the same closure**, while the shipped macOS/Vz closeout gate is the stricter measured target above (P50 under 800 ms, P95 ≤ 2× P50). The `< 50 ms` / sub-100 ms figures scope to the warm no-boot control path and raw resume signaling, not the full end-to-end memory restore. ADR-090 §2 was internally inconsistent (diagram `<100ms` vs prose "well under a second") and is now reconciled to match. Still deferred: a `bench residency-resume` harness + a force-park hook / idle override so the parked path is reachable without the 20-min TTL (tracked in Plan 205 §"Deferred follow-ups").

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 Vz dev-builder snapshot park/restore slice:** the explicit Vz builder parked-state mechanism is now wired for the stable dev-builder session. `mvm-build::vz_builder` computes `state.vzsave` / `<snapshot>.machine-id`, sends host-only `SAVE` over the persistent Vz supervisor control socket, verifies the snapshot pair, stops the supervisor, and can respawn from the persisted `SupervisorConfig` in `StartupMode::Restore` with a fresh gvproxy. `mvmctl dev park` snapshots/stops the Vz dev builder; the next `mvmctl dev up` restores an existing snapshot before cold-boot fallback; `dev status` reports `parked`; `mvmctl doctor`'s `builder residency` line reports `parked (snapshot present)` vs `parked (no snapshot)`; parser + JSON shape + doctor wording are tested. The later proof9 closeout completes the idle/live/OCI acceptance lane.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 invocation-driven persistent-builder keeper:** the libkrun `persistent-builder` session now records `last_activity_unix_secs` in its shared JSON session record (backward-compatible for old records). Build dispatch touches the timestamp before/after persistent use, the CLI writes the same `MVM_DATA_DIR`-aware path that `mvm-build` reads, and the next build invocation applies the resolved residency policy before routing. `MVM_RESIDENCY=cold` now actively stops a live libkrun persistent-builder session before falling back to single-shot; idle `Park` decisions on this snapshot-incapable path degrade to teardown. CI-only tests cover JSON compatibility, activity touching, fresh-warm keep, cold teardown, and snapshot-unavailable idle teardown. The later proof9 closeout completes the Vz idle-timeout/live timing and live-coupled OCI lane.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 Vz dev-builder auto-park on down:** the Vz dev-builder parked-state path is now transparent for the normal lifecycle: `mvmctl dev down` auto-parks a live non-reset Vz dev builder when the resolved residency policy keeps a persistent builder, while `--reset`, rebuild, cache-clear, cold residency, and failed restore paths remove stale `state.vzsave` markers so the next `dev up` cold-boots instead of accidentally waking an unwanted builder. The resume side is residency-gated too: `dev up` restores only when the current policy still allows a resident builder. Focused gating tests cover cold/no-live/reset/resume truth-table behavior. The later proof9 closeout completes the idle-timeout live timing and live-coupled OCI proof.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 Vz dev-builder invocation keeper:** the Vz dev-builder now has the same no-background-daemon keeper shape as the libkrun persistent-builder path. `dev up`, restore/reuse, and `dev shell` touch a `last-activity-unix-secs` marker in the Vz builder state dir; `dev status` applies the resolved residency policy before reporting state, parking a warm builder after the idle threshold, parking any live builder under `parked`, and tearing down under `cold`. `dev up` also enforces cold at entry, so a stale live Vz builder cannot be reused when the policy says cold boot. Unit coverage pins not-running keep, cold teardown, parked park, warm threshold behavior, and timestamp persistence. The later proof9 closeout completes the live macOS-26 no-boot/restore timing proof plus live-coupled OCI `run --image` proof.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 live-gate runner:** `scripts/capture-plan-205-live-gates.sh` captures the remaining macOS/Vz Plan 205 acceptance lane in one opt-in command. It builds or uses a supplied `mvmctl`, isolates state under `/tmp` by default, records per-command stdout/stderr plus `timings.tsv`, writes `summary.json`, asserts warm reuse, parked restore samples, cold teardown setup, and `run --image` residency coverage, and fails if command execution or the latency budgets regress. The later proof9 closeout is the green target-host evidence for this runner.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 205 complete:** the live-gate runner is green on the target macOS-26 Apple Silicon host. **Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 175 Task 1 (VMGenID delivery) host/unit landed:** the `PostRestore` resume RPC now carries the host-minted generation token end to end so a snapshot restore actually rotates the guest CSPRNG. `GuestRequest::PostRestore` became a struct variant `{ token: [u8; GENID_BYTES] }` (`#[serde(default)]` → all-zero = "no rotation" for template-restore callers), `PostRestoreAck` gained `reseeded: bool`, and the guest agent feeds the token to a process-resident `GenIdReseeder` (snapshot-captured, baseline-zero seed) via a new zero-aware `on_post_restore_token` dispatch wrapper; `GenIdState::new`/`GenIdReseeder::new` are now `const fn`. Both host senders mint a fresh token per resume (`VsockPostRestoreSignal`, `post_restore_at`); `mvmctl resume` surfaces "VMGenID rotated". Unit-proven (zero-token no-op, fresh-token rotate, idempotent re-send, two-clone divergence, wire round-trip + `deny_unknown_fields` + serde defaults); fmt+clippy clean. Step 3 (live snapshot→restore `/dev/urandom`-divergence assertion) stays gated — it rides Task 4's FC restore driver. T2 (UFFD/NBD/hugepages substrate), T3 (SIGUSR1 primed barrier), and T4 (`FirecrackerBackend::warm_start` + verb + `agent_ping` e2e) remain.

**Additional 2026-06-20 — Plan 118 Firecracker standby pool live:** the non-vz Firecracker standby box is implemented and live-validated.
